- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Gustavus Adolphus, leader of Sweden (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Sweden
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Hakkapeliitta, Replacing Cuirassier (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Carolean, Replacing Fusilier (Requires technology: Rifling)
        - Unique Building: Nobel Committee, Replacing Foreign Bureau (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: Bastu, Replacing Bath (Requires technology: Metal Casting)
        - Civilization Ability: Melee Land Units start with the Gå-På Promotion, and Siege Units start with the Mobility Promotion. All Military Units heal 10 HP and earn 2 XP when a Great Person is born. +15% Combat Strength from / Leadership.
      - Leader: Gustavus Adolphus
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      
      ## Policies
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        - Rationale: 4-turn countdown to cultural extinction requires absolute ruthlessness. Meanness 10 for zero compromise. WorkWithWillingness 8 (down from 9) maintains France alliance while ensuring primary focus on Iroquois destruction. WorkAgainstWillingness 10 for total anti-Iroquois coalition. Every action must prioritize crushing Iroquois tourism infrastructure over diplomatic niceties. Capitulated Iroquois cities have zero rights in this existential war.
        
        ## Technology

        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Mongolia
        - Public: 5
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Mongolia now our vassal - adjusting to appropriate peaceful relationship. Public at 5 signals friendly but subordinate stance for vassal state. Private at 0 maintains neutral attitude - they capitulated but may harbor discontent we must consider. No adjustment needed to our existing fruitful partnership.
        - UpdatedTurn: 341
        
        ### China
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -10
        - Rationale: Maintaining current relationship with China (our vassal). Public at 0 (neutral), Private at -10 to maintain strategic pressure while ensuring vassal compliance. China provides trade routes and resources but warrants some caution due to their former independence. Recent military upgrades continue under our guidance.
        - UpdatedTurn: 380
        
        ### Assyria
        - Public: -5
        - Private: -5
        - Rationale: Keeping relationship with Assyria at current hostile levels (-5/-5). Assyria is our vassal now (though game shows as capitulated under Netherlands), involved in ongoing war. Maintaining negative relationship reflects war status while avoiding excessive hostility. Military weakness makes them secondary priority.
        - UpdatedTurn: 380
        
        ### Poland
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Maximum hostility toward Poland following their capture of Katowice and destruction of 2 fighters and a Great General. This is now an existential conflict requiring total war - no peace until Katowice is liberated and Poland is crushed.
        - UpdatedTurn: 409
        
        ### France
        - Public: 25
        - Private: 25
        - Rationale: France is critical ally against Iroquois - they actively fight Iroquois (War Score -12%). Maintaining 25/25 ensures maximum coordination against Onondaga and Iroquois tourism infrastructure. Their 2039 military and Autocracy ideology make them our most valuable military partner for stopping Iroquois cultural victory in 4 turns.
        - UpdatedTurn: 434
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Iroquois 4 turns from cultural genocide of Sweden (97% Popular). Capture of Genesse River proves conquest works - must accelerate with maximum hostility. -100/-100 validates nuclear strikes on Onondaga and complete destruction of their tourism infrastructure. No diplomatic consideration overrides this extinction threat. Every delay brings cultural victory closer.
        - UpdatedTurn: 434
        
        ### The Netherlands
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Netherlands secondary cultural threat with 6 Influential civilizations and 80% Popular on Sweden. Current war exhaustion (9%) indicates active resistance, but Netherlands diplomatic penalty from capture remains acceptable. Public -100 signals total hostility; Private -50 maintains diplomatic flexibility if tactical situation changes. Prevent them from opening second front while focusing on Iroquois extinction threat.
        - UpdatedTurn: 434
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Iroquois 97% Popular - cultural victory imminent in 3-4 turns. Genesse River captured provides forward base for final offensive. Multiple Stealth Bombers nearly complete (Espoo: 2 turns, Linköping: 5 turns, Lund: 6 turns) will enable strategic bombing of Onondaga. Modern Armor and Rocket Artillery positioned at Genesse River must advance immediately toward Onondaga capital. Extreme offensive posture with 90% UseNuke remains necessary - nuclear strikes authorized if conventional offensive cannot destroy Onondaga before cultural extinction. Every military and air asset prioritized for Iroquois destruction. France alliance coordination continues on Iroquois front. No strategic diversion from this existential cultural threat.

              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Swedish Sphere of Influence: Jakarta:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Swedish Sphere of Influence: Genoa:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Swedish Sphere of Influence: Eraclea:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Holmul:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Zurich:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Assyria:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Buddhism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Sanction: The Netherlands:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - Proposer: Sweden
          - Enact: Sanction: Poland:

  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Gustavus Adolphus, leader of Sweden (Opponent 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Sweden
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Hakkapeliitta, Replacing Cuirassier (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Carolean, Replacing Fusilier (Requires technology: Rifling)
        - Unique Building: Nobel Committee, Replacing Foreign Bureau (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: Bastu, Replacing Bath (Requires technology: Metal Casting)
        - Civilization Ability: Melee Land Units start with the Gå-På Promotion, and Siege Units start with the Mobility Promotion. All Military Units heal 10 HP and earn 2 XP when a Great Person is born. +15% Combat Strength from / Leadership.
      - Leader: Gustavus Adolphus
      - OpponentID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      
      ## Policies
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Critical Situation:**
            - Iroquois is 97% Popular on Sweden and "3 turns to Influential" - this means cultural victory is IMMINENT.
            - The Iroquois has 6/7 Influential civs and is very close to winning culturally.
            - We MUST stop the Iroquois from becoming Influential over us within 3 turns.
            - Tourism/culture ratio is 323% - that's extremely high.
            
            **Our Military Position:**
            - Sweden has massive military strength: 16031 (by far the strongest).
            - We have Stealth Bombers being produced (Espoo: just completed, Linköping: 4 turns, Lund: 6 turns).
            - We have a forward base at Genesse River (captured from Iroquois).
            - Modern Armor and Rocket Artillery are positioned at Genesse River.
            - War with Iroquois is ongoing, war score 26% in our favor, but war weariness 45%.
            
            **Key Strategic Considerations:**
            1. The only way to stop cultural victory in 3 turns is to dramatically reduce Iroquois tourism output OR capture their capital/destroy their tourism infrastructure.
            2. The Iroquois capital Onondaga has MASSIVE wonders: Great Lighthouse, Great Library, Colossus, Hagia Sophia, Chichen Itza, Himeji Castle, Statue of Zeus, Temple of Artemis, Prora, Borobudur, Karlstejn, Olympic Village, etc. This city is producing huge tourism.
            3. Capturing Onondaga would remove a huge portion of their tourism output.
            4. We have Stealth Bombers now - we need to use them to bomb Onondaga and then capture it with Modern Armor.
            5. We also have nukes available (90% UseNuke flavor set last turn).
            
            **Diplomatic Situation:**
            - France is our ally (25/25) and fighting Iroquois too.
            - Netherlands is also a cultural threat (81% Popular on us, 16 turns to Influential) but less immediate.
            - Poland is hostile and captured Katowice, but we can't afford to divert from Iroquois.
            - We're at war with Iroquois and Poland.
            
            **Victory Path:**
            - Domination is our best alternative - we control 3/8 original capitals.
            - Science victory: Netherlands is ahead (33% parts, Apollo completed).
            - Cultural victory: We're only Influential over 2 civs. Iroquois is about to win culturally.
            
            **Immediate Actions Needed:**
            1. MUST capture Onondaga within 3 turns to stop cultural loss.
            2. Nuclear weapons may be necessary if conventional forces can't reach in time.
            3. Need to direct Stealth Bombers against Onondaga immediately.
            4. Keep max offensive posture.
            
            **Available Tools:**
            - The Options for Technologies, Policies, and Grand Strategies are limited.
            - Next tech: Nuclear Fusion (4 turns) - this unlocks Advanced Ballistics? Actually in VP, Nuclear Fusion unlocks Advanced Ballistics? No, Nuclear Fusion usually unlocks Manhattan Project and nukes? Actually in Civ V, Nuclear Fusion unlocks... wait, we already have Atomic era. Nuclear Fusion leads to Particle Physics? Not sure. But we have Electronics available as next tech choice.
            - Policies available: None specifically listed as "next" options except we can choose from the listed ones. But we should probably continue our current policy path or switch to something useful.
            
            Wait, looking at the Options section:
            - Technologies: Electronics is listed as available to set as next.
            - Policies: Various are listed including Futurism, Military-Industrial Complex, Lebensraum, New World Order, Iron Fist, Trade Confederacy, Ceremony, Consulates, Colonialism, Justice, Civilizing Mission, Sovereignty, Division of Labor, Free Trade, Fealty, Authority.
            
            But since we're in the middle of an existential crisis, I should focus on:
            1. Keep extreme offensive flavors
            2. Set research to something useful (maybe Electronics for Police Station? Or just continue current research)
            3. Maybe adjust relationships slightly if needed
            4. The key is the set-flavors tool or keep-status-quo
            
            Actually, looking at the current research: "CurrentResearch: Nuclear Fusion (Estimated in 4 turns)". Since Iroquois wins in ~3 turns, we don't have time to research something new. But Nuclear Fusion might unlock something important. However, the `set-research` tool says "Technology name to research next (None to clear)". Electronics is the only option explicitly listed in Options.
            
            Wait, is Electronics useful? It unlocks Police Station ( city security). Not immediately useful for stopping Iroquois.
            
            Actually, the most important factors right now:
            1. We HAVE Stealth Bombers. One was just completed in Espoo.
            2. We need ALL available air power hitting Onondaga.
            3. Modern Armor and Mechanized Infantry need to push from Genesse River toward Onondaga.
            
            But looking at military zones:
            - Enemy Land Zone 1062 (Onondaga): Enemy strength 3784, Friendly strength 117. Posture: Withdraw.
            - This means our units are being told to withdraw because the enemy looks too strong there, but that's WRONG for our strategic needs. We MUST attack Onondaga even at heavy cost.
            
            Wait, there's also Friendly Land Zone 19852 (Genesse River) with Modern Armor and Rocket Artillery, Mechanized Infantry, and sea units.
            
            The distance from Genesse River (X:2, Y:23) to Onondaga (X:27, Y:36) is quite far - about 25 tiles east and 13 tiles north. That's a massive distance. Our land army can't reach in 3 turns.
            
            HOWEVER, we have Stealth Bombers! Stealth Bombers have range 20? Let me think. In Civ V, Stealth Bombers have range 20. Linköping (X:64, Y:16) to Onondaga (X:27, Y:36) - distance is about 37 tiles. Too far.
            But wait, we have Stealth Bombers in Birka? No, Birka has no Stealth Bombers listed in zone 2051. Actually zone 2051 shows Stealth Bomber: 2 in Birka area.
            Birka is at X:69, Y:16. Onondaga is X:27, Y:36. That's sqrt((42)^2 + (20)^2) = 46 tiles. Way too far.
            
            Where can Stealth Bombers reach Onondaga from? We would need a city or carrier within 20 tiles of Onondaga.
            - Genesse River is at X:2, Y:23. Distance to Onondaga X:27, Y:36 = sqrt(25^2 + 13^2) = 28 tiles. Too far.
            - We have a Carrier in zone -19396 near Kaunas. Kaunas is X:10, Y:39. Distance to Onondaga X:27, Y:36 = sqrt(17^2 + 3^2) = 17 tiles! That's within range!
            - Wait, the Carrier is at Kaunas area? Looking at zone 19396 (Kaunas): Sweden units include Carrier, Battleship, Submarine, Rocket Artillery, Mechanized Infantry, Sensor Combat Ship.
            - If we have a Carrier near Kaunas (X:10, Y:39), and Stealth Bombers can base on carriers... wait, can Stealth Bombers base on carriers? In Civ V, Bombers and Stealth Bombers CAN base on carriers. Yes!
            - So we could move Stealth Bombers to the carrier near Kaunas and reach Onondaga!
            
            But do we have Stealth Bombers available? We just completed one in Espoo (X:49, Y:17). But Espoo is far from Kaunas.
            We also have Stealth Bombers in:
            - Zone 1061 (Stockholm): 1
            - Zone 2051 (Birka): 2
            - Zone 2728 (Turku): 1
            - Zone 3433 (Linköping): 1
            - Zone 4516 (Espoo): 2 (just completed one, maybe had one already?)
            Actually looking at zones again:
            - Stockholm zone: Stealth Bomber 1
            - Turku zone: Stealth Bomber 1
            - Linköping zone: Stealth Bomber 1
            - Espoo zone: Stealth Bomber 2
            - Birka zone: Stealth Bomber 2
            
            Total: 7 Stealth Bombers? That seems like a lot. Wait some zones might be overlapping or miscounted. Let me recount from the list:
            
            Zone 1061 (Stockholm): Stealth Bomber: 1
            Zone 2051 (Birka): Stealth Bomber: 2
            Zone 2728 (Turku): Stealth Bomber: 1
            Zone 3433 (Linköping): Stealth Bomber: 1
            Zone 4516 (Espoo): Stealth Bomber: 2
            
            Total = 7 Stealth Bombers.
            
            Stealth Bomber range is 20. Can any of our cities reach Onondaga?
            - Stockholm (61,11) to Onondaga (27,36): dx=34, dy=25 → ~42 tiles. No.
            - We need carriers forward deployed.
            
            Wait, there's a mention of "Modern Armor and Rocket Artillery positioned at Genesse River must advance immediately toward Onondaga capital." But those are land units and Onondaga is 28 tiles away. They can't reach in 3 turns. 
            
            Actually, looking at map coordinates more carefully:
            - Our closest units to Onondaga appear to be in the Genesse River area and potentially naval units.
            - Kaunas is at X:10, Y:39. Onondaga is X:27, Y:36. Distance = sqrt(289 + 9) = sqrt(298) = 17.3 tiles.
            - If we have a Carrier at Kaunas, and Stealth Bombers have range 20, we could bomb Onondaga from a carrier at Kaunas!
            - But can we get Stealth Bombers to Kaunas in time? Stealth Bombers move 2 tiles per turn (or rebase). Rebase range for air units is typically double their attack range, so 40 tiles for Stealth Bombers.
            - Espoo (49,17) to Kaunas (10,39): dx=39, dy=22 → ~45 tiles. Might be too far for rebase in one turn.
            - But we have multiple cities. Maybe Stockholm or Geneva to Kaunas?
            
            Actually, there's a simpler point: the staff members handle tactical unit movement. I set flavors and they execute. The key issue is that the tactical posture in the Onondaga zone is "Withdraw" because our local forces (117 strength) are overwhelmed by Iroquois (3784). We need to change that posture to "attack at all costs."
            
            But we can't directly order "attack Onondaga." We can only set flavors. The staff members will decide based on flavors and the strategic situation.
            
            We need:
            - Air: 100 (already set)
            - UseNuke: 90 (already set)
            - Offense: 100 (already set)
            - Mobilization: 100 (already set)

            In Civ V cultural victory:
            - For civ A to be Influential over civ B, civ A's total lifetime tourism to civ B must exceed civ B's total lifetime culture.
            - Tourists are generated each turn and "spent" against each civ's culture.
            - This is an accumulating pool. You don't lose accumulated tourism.
            
            Therefore, reducing future tourism generation does NOT affect the accumulated ratio. It only slows future progress. If they're at 97% now, they need 3 more percent. Even if we reduce their tourism to 0, the ratio stays at 97% forever and they never reach 100%.
